8:["$","p",null,{"children":["The ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA)"}]," is a hybrid quantum-classical iterative method for solving combinatorial optimization problems. In this tutorial, you will use QAOA to solve the ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"maximum-cut (max-cut)"}]," problem — an NP-hard optimization problem with applications in clustering, network science, and statistical physics. Given a graph of nodes connected by edges, the goal is to partition the nodes into two sets such that the number of edges crossing the partition is maximized."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]
9:["$","p",null,{"children":"Combinatorial optimization also appears in sustainability challenges, such as planning delivery routes to reduce fuel use and allocating resources to limit material waste. The max-cut example introduces the process of expressing an objective as a cost Hamiltonian and exploring candidate solutions with QAOA. Applying this approach to a sustainability problem requires an objective and constraints that represent the intended application.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

47:["$","p",null,{"children":"A smoothly decreasing curve that plateaus is the signature of convergence. A noisy, non-monotonic curve usually indicates that something upstream needs attention; common causes are too few shots per evaluation (high estimator variance), poor initial parameters, or a circuit whose depth is dominated by hardware noise. COBYLA is derivative-free and fairly robust to moderate noise, but when the noise swamps the actual cost improvements per step, its linear-approximation model can no longer tell real descent from random jitter and the optimizer wanders.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]
